It became pretty overwhelming, and I guess I realized I should talk to someone before I make it worse. At the time I told myself I was just being efficient, like sleep was kind of optional.  \n\nI know that probably sounds a little off when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling unusually upbeat, irritable, or overly confident during this time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I did feel pretty upbeat at first. Maybe more than usual. Like, almost… electrically upbeat, if that makes sense? I thought I was just excited about the assignment and confident in the ideas.\n\nBut then I got irritable too. Not, um, not trying to make it sound worse than it was, but if someone questioned my plans, I’d feel like they were slowing down something that was obviously important. I guess I was more sure of myself than I normally am. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how long you felt that way—like you were unusually upbeat or irritable? Was this a distinct period that lasted for a certain amount of time, or did it come and go?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it was a pretty distinct stretch, yes. I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s been kind of this intense swing from being in overdrive to… barely functioning, since I moved into the temporary apartment about three weeks ago.\n\nIt wasn’t just a mood for an afternoon or anything. It’s been going on across that whole period, though I may not have recognized it clearly at first. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Since you mentioned swinging from being in overdrive to barely functioning, have you felt persistently down or lost interest in things you usually enjoy during this time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I’ve been trying not to make too big a deal of it, because I know everyone gets stressed, but lately I wake up in the temporary apartment and there’s just this heavy, hollow feeling.\n\nIt’s like the day is already ruined before I even get in the car to drive to the plant. I don’t know if that counts as “persistently down,” exactly, but… it’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you felt down or lost interest in things for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but lately most days I wake up in the temporary apartment with this heavy, hollow feeling, like the whole day is already ruined before I even drive to the plant.\n\nIt’s been… pretty constant. Not just a bad morning here or there. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when your mood was extremely elevated or irritable, and you had so much energy or activity that it was clearly different from your usual self, not just feeling good about accomplishing things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that did happen, especially at the beginning of this assignment. I don’t want to make it sound like I was out of control or anything, because at first it felt almost productive—like I was just really energized and on top of things.\n\nBut it was more than just feeling good about work. I felt almost… electrically upbeat, and then I’d get weirdly irritable if someone questioned my ideas. Like they were slowing down something that seemed completely obvious and important to me. I guess that was pretty different from how I normally am.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt so unusually energetic, confident, or irritable that it was noticeable to others—even if it felt good or seemed like you were just being productive?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I didn’t see it that way at first, because it felt like I was just finally on top of everything.\n\nAt the beginning of this assignment I was almost… electrically upbeat, I guess. And then if people questioned my ideas, I got weirdly irritable, like they were slowing down something that seemed really obvious and important. I don’t know if it was as noticeable as you mean, but I think people could probably tell I wasn’t acting like my usual self.\n\nDoctor: Did this unusually upbeat or irritable mood last for at least a week without breaking, or did it come and go in shorter bursts?\n\nPatient: It wasn’t, um… exactly one clean week of feeling upbeat the whole time. It’s more like since I moved into the temporary apartment, about three weeks ago, I’ve been swinging between being in overdrive and then barely functioning.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the kind of duration you mean. It hasn’t felt normal, but it also hasn’t been one steady “up” mood without any break.\n\nDoctor: In the past, have you had a period lasting at least four full days in a row where you felt unusually high, euphoric, or irritable — not just a good mood or stress — where it was a constant feeling throughout the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate it into exactly four full days, if that makes sense. Since I moved into the temporary apartment, about three weeks ago, it’s felt like this intense shift has been happening—like I go from being in overdrive to barely functioning.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it or make it sound more dramatic than it is, but it hasn’t felt like my normal ups and downs. The pattern has been going on for those three weeks.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, was there a time when you felt unusually high, euphoric, or irritable—more than just a good mood or stress—where that feeling stayed with you all day, nearly every day, for at least four full days in a row?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, there was something like that. I know that sounds a little intense… but yes, it did feel like it stayed with me in that way.\n\nDoctor: Was there a time when others—like coworkers or family—commented that you seemed unusually upbeat, irritable, or not like your usual self?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little. I mean, I don’t think anyone said it in a dramatic way, but I could tell people noticed I was more “on” than usual at the beginning of the assignment.\n\nI felt almost electrically upbeat, like everything was clicking, and then I got… more irritable than I’d like to admit. If coworkers questioned my ideas, I felt like they were slowing down something that seemed obvious and important to me. I’m a bit embarrassed to say this, but it got to the point where I had to ask to be pulled out of a shift-planning meeting. I just wasn’t handling it properly. And occupational health got involved because I wasn’t really able to make the basic production decisions my role depends on.\n\nI know that sounds bad. Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s definitely been more than a week.

Since I moved into the temporary apartment about three weeks ago, things have felt really off — like I went from being in overdrive to barely functioning. The intensity has sort of shifted, but it hasn’t really felt like my usual self during that time. Does that answer what you mean?
